### AM-066: [Configure / LADT] Payment-phase display at the LEAGUE level — grid cell says "See age group level" for Deposit, and the ⓘ tooltip says "job default"
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-02) — split out from PL-062 (League-level payment-phase display fixes belong together as their own item)
- **Two display edits, both at the League level of the LADT editor:**
  1. **League grid PAYMENT PHASE cell reads "See age group level" for a Deposit-first league — should read "Deposit."** When the League card is **Full payment now**, the grid cell correctly shows **"PIF"**; when it's set to **Deposit first** (converted at league level), the cell falls through to **"See age group level"** instead of **"Deposit"** — even though every age group below correctly shows "Deposit." **Live repro (Ann, 08-02):** on **Top Threat Fall Draw 2026**, reset the League to Deposit first → grid still reads "See age group level." *Root cause:* the League-level Payment Phase cell (LADT sibling grid, `type:'phase'` renderer) handles the PIF stamp but resolves the league's own **Deposit** stamp as "inherit from below." *For Todd:* show **"Deposit"** when the league phase is deposit-first, mirroring the PIF path + the AG-level display.
  2. **ⓘ inheritance tooltip says "Inherited from job default" — should say "Inherited from League level."** The ⓘ icon's hover reads **"Inherited from job default"** when a fee/phase inherits from the job level; "job default" is unclear to Directors. **Ann: replace with "Inherited from League level"** (analogous to the Age Group hover text, and consistent with the new model where the **League card IS the top-level control**). *Where (code):* `sourceTooltip()` label map ([ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts:685-689](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts#L685)) — the `job: 'job default'` entry drives it. *For Todd:* relabel the `job` source so the ⓘ reads **"Inherited from League level"** (and "Set at League level" for the set case — pick wording that works in both the `Inherited from ${label}` and `Set at ${label} level` templates; e.g. map `job` → 'League' → "Inherited from League" / "Set at League level"). Reconcile with the existing `league` source label so the two don't read inconsistently.
- **Severity**: UX (LADT payment-phase display clarity at the League level)
- **Cross-ref**: **PL-062** (parent — the payment-phase "apply-to-all" retirement; these two display edits originated as PL-062 recommended-edit #1 + the tooltip follow-on, now split here), PL-060 (phase migration).
- **Status**: 🟢 **BUILT** (Todd go, 2026-08-02) — both parts closed, one already done before the item landed.
  - **Item 1 was already fixed** by `2c83471c` (08-02), built off the PL-062 verify note before this item was split out. Root cause was subtler than "resolves as inherit": a league on **Deposit first stores NO stamp by design** (null at the cascade top; only PIF writes a row), so the cell had nothing to render and fell to the fallback prose at [ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts:187](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts#L187). Fix synthesizes a display-only pill from the job baseline. **Not a new build — needs Ann's verify only.** Her 08-02 screenshot was taken on `v260801.1629.a3ffee67`, which predates the fix, so "See age group level" there is expected.
  - **Item 2 BUILT** `f8cc12cc` — [ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts:723](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts#L723). Todd: *"Ann is spot on"* — `job` now labels as **League**, per her "Inherited from League level". `job` and `league` deliberately collapse to one label; `agegroup`/`team` title-cased to match card names; "level" moved into the shared template so both branches read alike (**"Inherited from League level"** / **"Set at Team level"**). Codebase sweep confirmed this is the **only user-facing site** — the remaining "job-level" strings are internal comments describing the DB tier (and one non-rendering HTML comment in payment-tab), left accurate on purpose.
  - **Scope correction for verify:** the ⓘ renders **only inside a fee/phase pill**, never in an empty cell — a league row with all dashes has no icon at all. And "job default" was only ever reachable on the **FEES** pill: `source: 'job'` is seeded on the base fee entry ([ladt.component.ts:996](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.ts#L996), overwritten at [:1036](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.ts#L1036)), while the modifier/phase source unions are `'league' | 'agegroup' | 'team'` only ([:979-980](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.ts#L979)). So despite the payment-phase heading, the hover Ann saw was on a Fees pill.
  - **Ann verify post-deploy** (Fall Draw 2026): League card Deposit-first → league PAYMENT PHASE cell reads **"ClubRep: Deposit"**; flip to Full payment now → **"ClubRep: PIF"**; "See age group level" appears only where no fees exist below. Hover any ⓘ on a **Fees** pill → **"Inherited from League level"** (never "job default"); drill to an age group / team and confirm "Age Group" / "Team" wording. **Known and intended:** at league scope a job-baseline pill reads "Inherited from League level" while you're on the league row — correct under the League-is-the-top model, flag only if it reads wrong to you.
- **Also fixed alongside** (Todd, 08-02, `d213dfed`): league grid **HIDE CONTACTS / HIDE STANDINGS headers were clipping** to "HIDE CONTA…" / "HIDE STANDI…". Same AM-038 mechanic — header text gets `width − 38px` chrome (16px padding + 22px sort-icon reserve) before ej2's `.e-headertext` ellipsizes; 95px left a 57px box against ~72px for uppercase "STANDINGS". Both widened to **115px**. *Open, not fixed:* the league grid's "fits WITHOUT horizontal scroll" claim ([ladt-grid-columns.ts:28](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/configs/ladt-grid-columns.ts#L28)) is stale — columns total ~1260px and it scrolls; re-file if the scrollbar bothers Ann.
- **Item 1 — 🟢 BUILT (Todd, 2026-08-02, `2c83471c`)** — a deposit-first league stores no stamp by design (only PIF writes a row), so it fell through to the "See age group level" fallback; `buildFeeData` now synthesizes a league-scope pill (value = job baseline, source = job; twoPhase→"Deposit", none→"Single"). Display-only. ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-02)** — League set to Deposit first now shows the **"Deposit"** pill, matching the age groups; PIF still shows "PIF".
- **Item 2 — 🔴 STILL OPEN (tooltip not touched).** `sourceTooltip()` still maps `job: 'job default'` ([ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts:725](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/ladt-sibling-grid.component.ts#L725)). **Ann (08-02): the "Inherited from job default" text appears at BOTH the League AND Age Group levels** — the relabel to **"Inherited from League level"** must fix it in both places (it's the same `job`-source tooltip, so one label-map change covers both). *(Reinforced by item 1: the new synthesized league pills use `source = job`, so their ⓘ currently reads "Inherited from job default" too.)*
- **Status**: 🟡 **Item 1 ✅ VERIFIED (Ann, 08-02); Item 2 🔴 OPEN** — relabel `job` source tooltip "job default" → "League level" (shows at both League + Age Group levels). Back to Todd.

### AM-067: [Search/Teams] Default page size is too small (20) — start larger; prefer "All", or 250
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-02)
- **Request (Ann)**: the Search/Teams results grid defaults to **20 per page** and a Director **always has to increase it**. Recommend **showing ALL on lookup (preferred)**, or a **larger first setting like 250**. Effectively: drop the small 20/50 constraint as the starting view.
- **Where (code)**: [search-teams.component.ts:176](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/teams/search-teams.component.ts#L176) — `pageSettings: PageSettingsModel = { pageSize: 20, pageSizes: [20, 50, 100, 'All'] }`. Note **'All' already exists** in the options; the issue is purely the **default** (`pageSize: 20`).
- **For Todd**: change the default `pageSize` — either **'All'** (Ann's preference) or a larger number like **250**; and consider trimming the small options so the list starts big (e.g. `pageSizes: [100, 250, 'All']`, default 250, or default 'All'). **Perf note:** Search/Teams can be large (LFTC = 748 teams) — a Syncfusion grid with `allowPaging` still renders only the visible page, but 'All' materializes every row; **250 default is the safe middle ground**, 'All' is fine if the grid stays responsive on the biggest jobs. Todd's call on default vs the 'All' perf tradeoff.
- **⚠️ Precedent to reconcile**: **AM-026** accepted the **"All" page option as WON'T DO** on another grid, with an **Export-all workaround**. This is a *different* ask (raise the **default**, not add 'All' — 'All' already exists here) — but confirm the reasoning is consistent (why 'All'/large default is fine on Search/Teams if it was declined elsewhere; likely the team count is bounded per job vs. that grid's set).
- **Severity**: UX (Directors re-set the page size on every Search/Teams lookup)
- **Cross-ref**: AM-026 ("All" page option WON'T DO + Export-all, on a different grid).
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — built by Todd (2026-08-02, `5cf576ff`), [search-teams.component.ts:176](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/teams/search-teams.component.ts#L176). Todd's call: **match Search/Registrations** rather than pick a new number — `pageSize: 20 → 100`, `pageSizes: [20, 50, 100, 'All'] → [100, 500, 'All']`. Sub-100 options dropped so the list starts at the default, as Registrations does. **'All' kept** (it was already there and Ann values it); 1000 omitted as pointless when the largest known job is 748 teams and 'All' covers it.
  - **AM-026 precedent reconciled — the two grids differ mechanically, so this is not a reversal.** Registrations is **server-side paged** (`dataStateChange` fetches one page: [search-registrations.component.ts:244-246](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/search-registrations.component.ts#L244)), so "All" there meant pulling 10,606 rows over the wire *and* rendering them unvirtualized — the DOM bomb that resolution refused. **Search/Teams is client-side paged**: the grid binds to the in-memory result array with no `dataStateChange`, so **every matching row is already in the browser** when the search returns and page size controls DOM rendering only, never a fetch. Scale differs by an order of magnitude too (10,606 registrations vs ~748 teams on LFTC). Same reasoning, different mechanics, different answer.
  - **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)**: Search/Teams opens at 100 per page with no re-setting; picker offers 100 / 500 / All; 'All' renders and Export returns the full set on a large job.

### AM-068: [Team Reg / Club Teams Library] "Cancel" button on the register-existing-team row has no visible boundary — make it obviously a choice (keep Submit as default)
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-02)
- **Where**: Team registration → **Club Teams Library** fly-in → registering a team **already in the Library** (the inline register row). Buttons: **Cancel** ([library-flyin.component.ts:304](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/team/components/library-flyin.component.ts#L304), `.btn-register-cancel`) + **Submit / Save Changes** ([:305-309](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/team/components/library-flyin.component.ts#L305), `.btn-register-submit`).
- **What Ann saw**: the **Cancel** button's boundaries don't show — it reads as plain text, not an obvious clickable choice next to Submit.
- **Root cause (code)**: `.btn-register-cancel` ([:1145-1156](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/team/components/library-flyin.component.ts#L1145)) is styled **`border: none; background: none;`** with muted text (`--brand-text-muted`) — a borderless text button, so it has no visible boundary. `.btn-register-submit` is a solid primary button (correctly the default).
- **Request (Ann)**: make **Cancel a bit more prominent** so it's obvious it's a choice — give it a **visible boundary**. **Keep Submit as the default** (primary, as-is).
- **For Todd**: give `.btn-register-cancel` an **outlined secondary treatment** — a 1px border (design-system token, e.g. `--bs-border-color` / `--brand-border`) + optional subtle background, so it reads as a real (secondary) button. Keep it clearly **subordinate to the primary Submit** (Submit stays the obvious default action) — think `btn-outline-secondary` vs `btn-primary`. Existing `:focus-visible` box-shadow stays. Light/dark aware, WCAG AA.
- **Severity**: UX (Cancel not discoverable as a choice — borderless text button)
- **Cross-ref**: AM-042/047/064 (Ann's recurring "give it a visible boundary/prominence" theme — same class of fix, button context).
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — built by Todd (2026-08-02, `533f18cf`). `.btn-register-cancel` is now an **outlined secondary**: 1px `--bs-border-color`, transparent fill, muted text going full-strength with a `color-mix` tint on hover. The **underline hover was removed** — underline is link vocabulary and reads wrong inside a bordered button. Submit stays the solid primary/default, as Ann asked. Vertical padding 4px → 5px against Submit's 6px so the new 1px border lands both buttons on the same height; both added to the component's `prefers-reduced-motion` block (Submit had a transition but was missing from it).
  - **⚠️ Design-system finding — do NOT "modernize" these into Bootstrap classes.** The first pass at this converted both buttons to `.btn btn-sm btn-outline-secondary` / `.btn-primary` on the theory that Bootstrap is the house standard (238 `btn-primary` + 208 `btn-outline-secondary` usages app-wide). **Todd caught it and he was right.** Bootstrap is loaded **precompiled** ([angular.json:42](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/angular.json#L42) → `bootstrap.min.css`), so its button rules carry **literal `#0d6efd` / `#6c757d`** and never read `var(--bs-primary)` — the `--bs-primary: var(--brand-primary)` remap at [_tokens.scss:267](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/styles/_tokens.scss#L267) does not reach them. Our palette gets to `.btn-primary` in exactly two scoped places, both explicit workarounds: the wizard mixin ([_mixins.scss:7-32](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/styles/_mixins.scss#L7)) and dark mode ([_theme-dark.scss:238-249](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/styles/_theme-dark.scss#L238)). Converting Submit would have **regressed** it from brand-tracking `var(--bs-primary)` to Bootstrap blue. A comment now records this at the style block.
  - **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)**: Cancel shows a clear border and reads as a real choice, Submit stays the default; hover tints without underline; equal height; focus ring on both; light + dark OK.
- **➡️ Spawned finding (needs its own item, not built here)**: those **446 Bootstrap button usages** (238 `btn-primary` + 208 `btn-outline-secondary`) render **off-palette in light mode outside the wizard** — Bootstrap's blue/gray, unresponsive to all 8 palettes. The two scoped overrides above are evidence this was hit and patched locally twice instead of globally. A single global rule re-pointing `--bs-btn-*` at brand tokens (mirroring what `_theme-dark.scss` already does for dark mode) would bring all 446 into the palette at once. 🅿️ **PARKED (Todd, 2026-08-02): "we will NOT touch the palette issue at this stage."** Recorded for later only — do not file, sweep, or re-propose. Nothing about AM-068 depends on it.

### AM-069: [Configure / LADT] Payment-phase change — reconsider "Convert" vs "Save" button order/labels so "Save" reads as the completing action
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-02)
- **Ann's point**: for a **Payment Phase** change, the **"Convert"** and **"Save"** buttons may read better in the **opposite order**. **"Save" implies you've completed the process**, so the **final** action (the one that shows **who is affected**) should read as **"Save"** — that ordering will make sense to a Director. As Ann sees it: the yellow **Convert** should be first, and the **final** step (with the affected list) should be **Save**.
- **Current implementation (code)**: on a phase flip the sticky save bar switches inline to a confirm ([reprice-confirm.component.ts:31-32](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/reprice-confirm.component.ts#L31)) showing a **yellow "Convert"** (autofocus, retroactive — carries the affected count) + **"Cancel"**. Per the component's design the decision "appears in place at the point of the **Save** action," so today **"Convert" is effectively the completing click that comes *after* Save** — the reverse of what Ann finds intuitive (she expects "Save" = done to be the last button, not "Convert").
- **For Todd + Ann to align** (label/order UX — confirm exact intent before coding): options — (a) **relabel the final/completing button "Save"** instead of "Convert" (Convert = save-and-apply-retroactively, so "Save" reads as completion) while keeping the affected-count on it; and/or (b) **reorder** so the completing "Save" is the last button the Director clicks. Keep the yellow/warning emphasis on whichever is the go-action; keep Cancel as the clear secondary (see AM-068). *(Recording Ann's observation — the swap direction/labels are a joint call, not a silent code change, since the wording leaves current-vs-desired slightly open.)*
- **Severity**: UX (button-order/label clarity on a money-affecting confirm)
- **Cross-ref**: PL-062 #2 (Convert-button clarity — "click Convert to apply this phase to existing registrations" + auto-focus; this item is the sibling order/label question), AM-051 (one-Save-affordance), AM-068 (Cancel prominence).
- **Status**: 🔴 **WON'T DO — the button stays "Convert"** (Todd, 2026-08-02, after reviewing the change in place). Built as Ann asked (`0d6123b5`, label + the callout sentence naming it), Todd reviewed it and **reverted** (`1e38d010`). Net effect on the codebase: **none** — [reprice-confirm.component.ts:39](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/reprice-confirm.component.ts#L39) still reads **Convert**, and the PL-062 callout still says *"Click **Convert** to apply this phase to existing registrations — Cancel reverts the change."* **Nothing for Ann to verify here.** — ✅ **Ann reviewed + ACCEPTS the WON'T DO (2026-08-05)**: "Convert" stays (it reprices existing registrations retroactively; the two-step shape Ann described was the amount-change flow, not the phase flip). No change.
- **Why "Convert" is the right word** (for the conversation with Ann): this button doesn't just persist a setting — it **reprices existing registrations retroactively**. "Save" reads as "store what I typed"; "Convert" names the thing that actually happens to registrants who already paid. The yellow warning styling and the affected-count message are calibrated to that meaning.
- **The flow fact worth telling Ann**, since her ask rests on it: on a **phase flip** the confirm appears the moment she flips the radio, not after a separate Save click, and **Convert *is* the save** — the ordinary Save button is swapped out while the confirm is showing ([agegroup-detail.component.ts:601-613](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/agegroup-detail.component.ts#L601), [:206-214](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/components/agegroup-detail.component.ts#L206)). She isn't missing a second step. If **no** existing registrations are affected, no confirm appears at all and she just clicks the normal Save. The two-buttons-in-sequence shape she described is the **amount**-change flow, where the confirm does follow a Save click. If anything still bothers her after that, it's most likely the Save button vanishing on flip — which is a different item, not a label.

### AM-070: [Search/Registrations → Club Rename] Rename Club modal with a long affected-jobs list runs off-screen — Save/Cancel unreachable; also make the modal moveable
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-02)
- **Where**: Search/Registrations → registrant fly-in → **Rename Club** (SuperUser). The modal ([registration-detail-panel.component.html:740-782](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/components/registration-detail-panel.component.html#L740)) shows a warning + the list of scheduled jobs whose game names will be rewritten.
- **What Ann saw**: renaming a club (AhearnT → "Heros Lax") with teams in **24 scheduled jobs** grew the modal so tall it ran off the bottom of the screen. The **New club name** field, **Cancel**, and **Rename Club** buttons were pushed below the viewport, nothing scrolled, and the popup couldn't be moved — so there was **no way to proceed with (or cancel) the rename**.
- **Root cause (code)**: `.change-job-modal` ([.scss:991](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/components/registration-detail-panel.component.scss#L991)) was `position:absolute; top:30%` with **no `max-height` and no internal scroll**, and the impact list ([.scss:379](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/components/registration-detail-panel.component.scss#L379)) was unbounded — so a long list pushed the `.modal-footer` (Cancel / Rename Club) off the bottom.
- **Request (Ann)**: (1) **visibility** — always keep the buttons reachable no matter how long the job list; (2) **moveability** — let the popup be dragged out of the way.
- **Part 1 — Visibility: 🟢 FIXED (Claude, 2026-08-02, working tree — not yet committed/deployed).** Bounded the shared modal to the viewport and made it a flex column so header + footer stay pinned while the body scrolls: `.change-job-modal` gets `max-height: calc(100% - --space-16)`, `display:flex; flex-direction:column; overflow:hidden`, vertically centered (`top:50%` + `translate(-50%,-50%)`); `.modal-body` gets `overflow-y:auto; flex:1 1 auto; min-height:0`; `.modal-header`/`.modal-footer` get `flex:0 0 auto`. Footer buttons now always visible; long list scrolls inside the modal. (Shared class — the "Change Job" modal gets the same overflow safety.) ✅ **Rename function itself verified working by Ann (2026-08-02).**
- **Part 2 — Moveability: ✅ RESOLVED 2026-08-05 (Todd go).** The modal is now draggable by its header.
  - **The deferral premise was wrong** — "no drag machinery on these modals today" is false. [`appDraggableModal`](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/shared-ui/directives/draggable-modal.directive.ts) already existed (header-handle drag, viewport clamping, interactive controls in the header never start a drag) and was already live on **refund-modal** and **batch-email-modal — both in this same registrant fly-in** — plus the accounting ledger. Part 2 was far cheaper than filed.
  - **The one real obstacle, and the fix:** `.change-job-modal` centered itself with `transform: translate(-50%, -50%)`, and the directive drives movement by writing `transform` on that same element — first drag would have wiped the centering and jumped the modal. Centering moved to the **independent `translate` property** ([:995](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/components/registration-detail-panel.component.scss#L995)); individual transform properties apply *before* `transform`, so the two compose — centered at rest, offset by the drag. A code comment guards it. **Do not move centering back into `transform`.**
  - **Both modals on that class get it** — Rename Club AND Change Job ([:707](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/components/registration-detail-panel.component.html#L707), [:745](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/components/registration-detail-panel.component.html#L745)) — they share `.change-job-modal`. Position resets to centered on every reopen for free (the modals live inside an `@if`, so the directive is re-created).
  - Side effect, benign: the open `fadeIn` keyframe animates `transform: translateY()`, which previously *overrode* the centering for those 200ms (the modal faded in off-centre). Now it composes, so the open animation is centred too.
  - Part 1's internal scroll is untouched — Ann's 08-04 verify still stands.
- **Severity**: 🔴 Blocking when triggered (couldn't complete/cancel the rename) → mitigated by Part 1; Part 2 is UX polish.
- **Cross-ref**: AM-060 (same registrant fly-in), PL-062 (LADT confirm-in-place modal pattern).
- **Status**: ✅ **CLOSED — both parts VERIFIED.** Part 1 **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — overflow fix (list scrolls inside; Cancel / Rename Club reachable), `e961baeb`. **Part 2 (draggable) ✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-05)** — modal drags by its title bar, stays on screen, ✕/name-field still work, resets centered on reopen; Change Job too. Built by Todd 2026-08-05.

### AM-071: [Configure / Job Clone → Dates] Expiry defaults are wrong — Admin should outlive User by a year, and both should land end-of-month
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-03) — Job Clone walkthrough
- **Where**: Job Clone workbench → **Dates** panel → **Admin expiry** + **User expiry** ([job-clone-workbench.component.html:96-106](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L96)).
- **What Ann saw / wants**: the two expiry defaults should not be identical. **Admin expiry should default one year later than User expiry** (admins need access after the public season closes), and **both dates should default to end-of-month** (clean season-boundary dates, not a mid-month day).
- **Root cause (code) — SUPERSEDED, see Status.** *(As filed: both fields seeded to the same `today + 1 year` value, mid-month, no end-of-month rounding and no admin/user offset. That was true of the build Ann tested but not of HEAD — `ffbb1c17` (2026-08-03 14:21) replaced it with a source-anchored shift.)*
- **For Todd**: change the default seeding so **User expiry = end-of-month of (today + 1 year)** and **Admin expiry = end-of-month of (User expiry + 1 year)** — i.e. Admin outlives User by a full year, both snapped to the last day of their month. Director can still override either in the picker. (Values flow through `ExpiryAdmin`/`ExpiryUsers` on the request → `JobCloneResetRules` [:69-70](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneResetRules.cs#L69); the change is to the **frontend defaults**, unless you'd rather compute defaults server-side in the plan.) Confirm the desired anchor with Ann if "today" vs "source-job expiry + 1 season" matters — this item assumes today-anchored.
- **Severity**: UX / correctness (default dates)
- **Cross-ref**: PL-031 (General-tab Expiration split — User vs Admin lifecycles), AM-072/073 (same Job Clone pass).
- **Status**: 🟢 **CLOSED — no code (Todd, 2026-08-04)** — ✅ **ACKNOWLEDGED (Ann, 2026-08-04)** (accepts the no-code close, incl. the declined Admin=User+1yr / end-of-month normalization; expects a redeploy to clear the original stale-build complaint). Two findings:
  1. **Already fixed before this item was ruled on.** `ffbb1c17` (08-03 14:21) changed the seed from `today + 1 year` to **source job's own expiry + 1 year** ([job-clone-workbench.component.ts:387-388](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.ts#L387)). Ann's screen showed the old behavior because **staging was built 08-03 06:28 — eight hours before the fix**. A redeploy resolves her complaint on any source job that already follows the convention, which is most of them.
  2. **Normalization declined.** Probe of all 1,066 jobs carrying both dates: **750** already Admin = User + 1 yr, **25** identical, **291** some other gap, **993** land end-of-month. Todd's call: **leave it** — the clone carries the source's expiry relationship forward as-is; it does not impose the Admin = User + 1 yr / end-of-month house rule. The 316 non-conforming source jobs propagate their shape to their clones by design; the director edits either picker in the workbench before committing.
- **Known fallback (not a defect, recorded so it isn't re-diagnosed)**: `shiftYear` falls back to `new Date()` when the source date is absent/unparseable ([:612-614](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.ts#L612)) — that fallback *is* today + 1 year, and it is indistinguishable on screen from a real seeded date. It cannot fire for these two fields today (`JobCloneSourceDto` types both non-nullable; all 1,066 live jobs have both set). Also `setFullYear(+1)` rolls **Feb 29 → Mar 1**.
- **Blocks Ann's re-verify**: staging predates every Job Clone change made 08-03 afternoon onward, so this item — and every other Job Clone entry in the AM-075→081 batch — reads against stale code until a redeploy.

### AM-072: [Configure / Job Clone → Options] Drop the eCheck-specific toggle — too granular; just carry forward the source job's payment structure
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-03) — Job Clone walkthrough
- **Where**: Job Clone workbench → the **eCheck payments** option group ("Start turned off" / "Match the source job") ([job-clone-workbench.component.html:215-235](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L215)).
- **What Ann said**: singling out **eCheck** on the clone screen is **too specific** — a Director shouldn't have to make an eCheck-only decision here. The clone should just **bring the prior job's payment structure forward** (carry it as configured on the source), not surface one payment method as its own choice.
- **Current behavior (code)**: the wizard exposes a dedicated `enableEcheckChoice` radio (`off` | `source`) ([.ts:136](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.ts#L136)); the backend applies it in `JobCloneResetRules` [:130-132](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneResetRules.cs#L130) (`BEnableEcheck = choice=="source" && source.BEnableEcheck`), default **off** ([JobCloneDtos.cs:96-98](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.Contracts/Dtos/JobClone/JobCloneDtos.cs#L96)). Rationale for the current "start off" default was bank-detail safety (new season's bank info unconfirmed).
- **For Todd + Ann to align** (design decision, not a silent change): remove the eCheck-specific UI and **carry the source payment structure forward wholesale**. Open questions to settle before coding: (a) does "bring forward payment structures" mean **copy `source.BEnableEcheck` as-is** (drop the "start off" safety default)? (b) is there a broader "payment setup" (CC/eCheck/processing fees) that should carry as one unit rather than field-by-field? (c) keep the informational hint about confirming new-season bank details somewhere (e.g. Configure → Job) even after the toggle goes away. Note the eCheck **processing-fee** carry-forward is separate and already handled (`ResolvedEcheckProcessingFeePercent`, [JobClonePlanner.cs:422](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobClonePlanner.cs#L422)).
- **Severity**: UX / scope (over-specific control on the clone screen)
- **Cross-ref**: AM-071/073 (same Job Clone pass); the eCheck fee tests in `JobCloneServiceTests` would need updating if the choice is removed.
- **Status**: 🔴 **WON'T DO — the eCheck choice stays (Todd, 2026-08-04).** Ruled against on impact: removing it is not the copy-only edit it looks like — it touches 8 files (FE signal + two radios + request field, `JobCloneRequest.EnableEcheckChoice`, the `ValidEnableEcheckChoices` guard at [JobClonePlanner.cs:34,53-54](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobClonePlanner.cs#L53), the reset rule at [JobCloneResetRules.cs:138-140](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneResetRules.cs#L138), two `JobCloneServiceTests` cases, API model regen) **and flips a safety default**: today the choice defaults `off`, so eCheck arrives disabled on every clone; delete it and `BEnableEcheck` follows the source, meaning a clone of an eCheck job is born taking eChecks against **last season's unconfirmed bank details**. Options offered and declined: (1) hide the radio but keep `off` server-side — Ann's screen simplification with zero behavior change; (2) hide it and follow the source — her literal ask, accepting the bank risk. Todd chose to **leave the control as-is**; the hint added by `787dc1ca` already explains that the eCheck choice is inert when Payment Methods is CC-only. **Nothing for Ann to verify.** — ✅ **ACKNOWLEDGED (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — accepts keeping the eCheck control as-is (bank-safety default understood).

### AM-073: [Configure / Job Clone → "What arrives switched off"] Help text points to a deleted **Release page** and non-existent wizard steps — rewrite + tighten
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-03) — Job Clone walkthrough. *Ann also noted she likes the single-page plan overall.*
- **Where**: Job Clone workbench → the **"What arrives switched off"** explainer panel ([job-clone-workbench.component.html:408-452](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L408)).
- **What Ann saw**: the text tells the Director to "release the site and open registration from the **Release page**, which opens as soon as the clone finishes" ([:448-452](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L448)) — but **she never got a Release page**. She thinks the "steps" the copy references are pointing at it.
- **Root cause (code/history)**: the **Release page was deleted** in `ee4c9d6f` (2026-08-03) — the clone now switches straight into the new job's home page. The panel copy was **not updated to match**, so it still references the removed page *and* dead step numbers ("release it in **step 2**", "activate them in **step 3**", "open them in **step 4**" — [:416-422](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L416)) from the old multi-step release sequence. Where each release job actually moved (per `ee4c9d6f`): **activate admins → Configure → Administrators** (per-row toggle / Activate All); **open registration → Configure → Job** (registration flags); **public listing/cross-sell visibility (`bSuspendPublic`) → Mobile & Store**; **schedule/roster visibility → Configure → Job** (already stated correctly).
- **For Todd**: rewrite the closing "to turn these on" paragraph to point to the **real** current locations (above) and **drop the "step N" references** (there is no numbered wizard anymore — it's the single-page plan). While rewriting, **verify each "switched off" claim is still true** — e.g. the "public site is hidden until you release it" line ([:416-417](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L416)) leaned on `bSuspendPublic`, which per `ee4c9d6f` **does not gate a job's own pages** (only TSIC-Events listing + cross-sell) — so that claim may be overstated. Also **tighten the prose** per Ann (the panel is wordy).
- **Severity**: 🟠 Docs/UX — actively misleading (directs Director to a page that no longer exists)
- **Cross-ref**: `ee4c9d6f` (release-page deletion, where each function moved), AM-071/072 (same Job Clone pass).
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — the panel was rewritten. Confirmed in code: **no "Release page"** and **no "step 2/3/4"** references remain; the panel is now **"What you need to turn on"** naming real locations (Configure → Quick Links / Administrators / Discount Codes, Job Settings → Teams/ClubReps, Configure → Job). *(The old `<h3>` "What arrives switched off" section heading remains — harmless title, not the misleading copy.)*

### AM-074: [Registration wizard → Review screen] Should the uploaded headshot be shown on the Review screen? — decision for Todd
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-03) — Adult Registration walkthrough
- **Question (Ann)**: on the registration **Review** screen (the final summary before submit), should the registrant's **uploaded headshot be displayed** so they can confirm the photo they attached?
- **Current behavior (code)**: the headshot **upload** control lives on the **account/login step** ([adult account-step.component.ts:208-210](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/adult/steps/account-step.component.ts#L208), team [login-step.component.ts:381-383](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/team/steps/login-step.component.ts#L381)), but the **Review step does not echo it back** — grep for headshot/photo/avatar in [adult review-step.component.ts](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/adult/steps/review-step.component.ts) and [team review-step.component.ts](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/team/steps/review-step.component.ts) returns nothing. So the photo is captured but never shown on Review.
- **For Todd (decision)**: do you want the headshot rendered on Review (adult + team, and by extension player/family review screens)? If yes, it's a display-only add on the review card.
- **Implementation notes (if approved)**: two capture modes exist — **authenticated mode** (headshot already uploaded to statics as `{userId}.jpg`, show via the statics URL) and **create mode** (new adult, photo held as `headshotBase64` in wizard state until the account is minted at submit — [adult-wizard-state.service.ts:94-99](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/registration/adult/state/adult-wizard-state.service.ts#L94)). A Review preview should use the **base64** in create mode (works everywhere) and the statics URL in authenticated mode. ⚠️ Note the statics-URL path **won't render on dev/staging** (headshots read from the prod statics host — see the reference note / earlier headshot investigation 2026-08-03), so test the statics-mode preview on prod; the base64 create-mode preview works in all envs.
- **Severity**: Question / UX enhancement (not a bug)
- **Cross-ref**: headshot dev/staging display artifact (2026-08-03 investigation), AM-060 (registrant fly-in headshot display).
- **Status**: 🅿️ **PARKED — Todd + Ann agree (Ann accepted the park 2026-08-05).** "Not essential to go-live review"; not declined — deferred past cutover. Revisit post-launch only if Todd or Ann reopens it.
  - **Investigation banked (2026-08-05) so this doesn't get re-derived:**
    - **Confirmed**: no review-step on ANY wizard echoes the headshot — grep for headshot/photo/avatar across the adult, team and player review components returns nothing. The photo is captured and never shown back.
    - **Two capture modes drive the implementation**: *create mode* holds the image as `headshotBase64` in wizard state until the account is minted at submit (renders in every environment); *authenticated mode* reads it from statics as `{userId}.jpg`.
    - **The blocker to plan around**: statics-hosted headshots **do not render on dev or staging** (they read from the prod statics host). Since [[Ann verifies locally]], she can prove the create-mode preview but **cannot verify the authenticated-mode preview at all** — that half is provable only by Todd, on prod.
    - **Recommended shape when it thaws**: show in both modes, and hide the block on image load error — off-prod the authenticated preview then renders *nothing* instead of a broken-image icon, which is what makes it safe to ship. Display-only; no state or submit changes.
    - **Open scope question, unanswered**: Ann raised it on **Adult**. Adult only, or swept across Team and Player Review in the same pass? (Todd's standing rule — fix a pattern on every sibling surface — argues for all three.)

### AM-075: [Configure / Job Clone → Dates] One catch-all hint is confusing — split into an Expiry hint and an Event-dates hint
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-04) — Job Clone walkthrough
- **Where**: Job Clone workbench → **Dates** panel ([job-clone-workbench.component.html:96-114](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L96)). Four date fields in one grid: **Admin Expiry**, **User Expiry**, **Event Start**, **Event End**.
- **What Ann saw**: a single hint over all four ([:98-99](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L98)) — *"All four are seeded from the source job plus one year. Leave an event date blank to keep the shifted source value."* — is confusing, because the two expiry dates and the two event dates behave differently and shouldn't share one caption.
- **Request (Ann)** — replace the single hint with **two hints, each directly above its own pair**:
  1. **Above the Expiry pair** (Admin/User Expiry): *"Expiry Dates are seeded from the source job plus one year."*
  2. **Above the Event Start / Event End pair**: *"Enter Event Start and Event End only for tournaments. Player sites will automatically seed the team's dates from the source job plus one year."*
  *(Ann's wording, typos corrected: "tournaments", "automatically".)*
- **For Todd**: split the single `.jc-grid` into two labeled groups so each hint sits immediately above the correct pair (expiry group, then event-dates group), and drop the old catch-all line. Keep the existing event-date validation banners (`eventEndInPast`, `eventDatesInverted`, [:119-132](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L119)) under the event pair. Confirm the "tournaments vs player sites" copy matches actual behavior — i.e. whether the event window is only meaningful/seeded for tournament jobs (Ann's mental model); adjust wording if player sites also carry an event window.
- **Severity**: UX / docs (confusing field hint on the clone screen)
- **Cross-ref**: AM-071 (expiry-default seeding — same Dates panel; if AM-071 changes Admin=User+1yr end-of-month, keep this hint's "plus one year" wording consistent), AM-073 (same Job Clone copy-tightening pass).
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT (layout, verbatim copy)** but 🔴 **REOPENED for a copy revision (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — the Event-dates hint gets Ann's revised wording (per Todd's accuracy flag); see the REOPEN note at the end of this item. *(Original build: Todd go, 2026-08-04 — "make her edits".)* The single `.jc-grid` is now two `.jc-date-group` blocks, each with its own hint directly above its pair ([job-clone-workbench.component.html:96-125](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L96)): *"Expiry Dates are seeded from the source job plus one year."* over Admin/User Expiry, and *"Enter Event Start and Event End only for tournaments. Player sites will automatically seed the team's dates from the source job plus one year."* over Event Start/End. The old catch-all line is gone; both `eventEndInPast`/`eventDatesInverted` banners moved **inside** the event group so they stay with the pair they describe. One new rule (`.jc-date-group + .jc-date-group` margin). Layout + copy only — no `ngModel`, handler or validation change.
- **Copy correction recorded, not applied (Todd's call — ship Ann's words)**: the second hint doesn't match what those two fields do. They write **the job's own event window** (`EventStartDate`/`EventEndDate`), not "the team's dates", and they are **not tournament-only** — on any job type the event end feeds the `EventConcluded` hierarchy, so a player site whose director follows this hint and leaves them alone still gets the source's dates shifted +1 year, and an end date that lands in the past makes the new job **born concluded with no registration links** (the very banner sitting under these fields). The hint tells a player-site director the fields are not their concern when the second half of the sentence is what actually protects them. Revisit wording if directors act on it.
- **🔴 REOPENED (Ann, 2026-08-04) — revised Event-dates hint per Todd's accuracy flag.** Ann accepts the concern and chose new wording. **Replace** the event-group hint (*"Enter Event Start and Event End only for tournaments. Player sites will automatically seed the team's dates from the source job plus one year."*) **with:**
  > **"Leave blank to shift the source's event dates forward one year. Check that Event End is in the future — on any job type, a past end date opens the new job concluded (no registration links)."**
  - **For Todd**: swap only the **event-group** hint text at [job-clone-workbench.component.html:96-125](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job-clone/job-clone-workbench.component.html#L96) to the above. The **Expiry hint is unchanged.** This fixes both inaccuracies (not tournament-only; it's the job's own event window, not "the team's dates") and makes the hint agree with the `eventEndInPast` banner sitting right below it. Copy-only, no behavior change.

### AM-076: [Data cleanup / League names] HTML-entity-encoded apostrophes & ampersands in `Leagues.leagues.leagueName` (legacy residue) — normalize the 19 rows
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-04) — surfaced on the Job Clone → League names panel
- **What Ann saw**: cloning a Hero's Lacrosse job, the League names row rendered **`Hero&#39;s Tryouts 2026-2027 → Hero&#39;s Tryouts 2027-2028`** instead of an apostrophe.
- **Root cause (verified, not a clone bug)**: the value is stored **HTML-entity-encoded in the DB** — `Leagues.leagues.leagueName` literally contains the characters `Hero&#39;s …`. The clone screen renders the raw string faithfully (Angular text interpolation `{{ row.sourceName }}` + `[ngModel]` on the input — neither decodes entities), so the clone is *displaying* the artifact, not creating it. Confirmed on Staging `TSICV5`.
- **Scope (verified 2026-08-04, Staging TSICV5)**: **isolated to `Leagues.leagues.leagueName` — 19 rows.** All other name columns are clean: `Jobs.Jobs.jobName` = 0, `Leagues.teams.teamName` = 0, `Leagues.agegroups.agegroupName` = 0, `Clubs.Clubs.ClubName` = 0. The 19 are all `&#39;` (apostrophe, the Hero's leagues) or `&amp;` (ampersand). Because every *other* name column is clean, the **new write path does not encode** — this is pure legacy residue from the old system, with **no ongoing source**.
  - The 19: Hero&#39;s Camps and Clinics {2022,2024,2025,2026}; Hero&#39;s Inc Summer Skills Teams 2026; Hero&#39;s Players {2021-2022, 2025-2026, 2026-2027}; Hero&#39;s Tryouts {2021-2022 … 2026-2027}; LI Yellow Jackets Clinics &amp; Leagues {2021-2022, 2022-2023}; SJAA Running &amp; Cross Country Fall 2023; STEPS Ally Mastroianni Camps &amp; Clinics 2022-2023; Triple Threat Camps &amp; Clinics 2022.
- **Impact**: renders as literal `&#39;`/`&amp;` anywhere the name is shown as **text** (clone screen, inputs, emails, exports). On the clone it also **propagates** — the year-increment (`IncrementYearsInName`, [JobClonePlanner.cs:193-195](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobClonePlanner.cs#L193)) bumps the year but keeps the entity, so a cloned league is born with the same artifact unless the operator hand-edits the field.
- **Recommendations (for Todd)**:
  1. **One-time data normalization** of the 19 rows — decode `&#39;`→`'`, `&amp;`→`&` (and `&quot;`→`"` for completeness) in `Leagues.leagues.leagueName`. Low risk: one column, 19 rows, no ongoing writer. Preview before/after, run on Staging first, spot-check the clone screen, then apply the **same fix to Production at/around go-live** (these rows exist in prod too). *(Claude can write the preview + `UPDATE` script on request.)*
  2. **No code change required** for the source of the data (new write path is already clean). Optionally, if any surface is expected to *ever* receive user-entered `&`/`'` again, consider decoding-on-read or a normalize-on-save guard — but current evidence says it's unnecessary.
  3. **Interim, per-clone**: operators can just hand-edit the League-names field to a real apostrophe before committing a clone of an affected job (Hero's). Cleanup #1 makes this unnecessary going forward.
- **Severity**: 🟡 Data cleanup (display correctness; propagates through clone) — not blocking.
- **Cross-ref**: AM-073/075 (same Job Clone screen), go-live checklist (apply the normalization to Production).
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — fixed in the CLONE, not by a data script (Todd, 2026-08-04); Ann accepts leaving the 19 existing rows. The original recommendation here was a one-time `UPDATE` of the 19 rows. **Todd rejected that as the wrong shape**: the clone is the thing that re-mints the artifact, so cleaning rows behind it only holds until the next clone runs. The decode now happens where the name is written.
  - New `DecodeLegacyEntities` ([JobCloneTransforms.cs:63](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneTransforms.cs#L63)) — `WebUtility.HtmlDecode`, skipped entirely for `&`-free strings, applied at three points in `JobClonePlanner`: **before** the year bump so the pre-filled target is clean ([:193-199](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobClonePlanner.cs#L193)); on the **request** rename row, which is the value that becomes `Leagues.leagueName` via `JobCloneService` → `CloneLeague` → [JobCloneResetRules.cs:538](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneResetRules.cs#L538); and on `LeaguePlanDto.SourceName` so the workbench stops rendering `Hero&#39;s` literally (Angular text interpolation doesn't decode entities).
  - The "name unchanged from the source" warning now compares against the **decoded** source — comparing raw would have silenced it on exactly the 19 affected leagues.
  - **Build**: `TSIC.API` clean, 0 warnings / 0 errors.
  - **The 19 EXISTING rows: LEAVE THEM (Todd, 2026-08-04 — "not important"). No data script, on Staging or Production, now or at go-live.** This fix stops propagation only; those rows keep rendering `&#39;`/`&amp;` on their own jobs and that is accepted. They age out as those jobs are cloned forward. **Do not re-propose a normalization `UPDATE`.** (Counts for the record, SELECT probes only: 19 rows — 14 `&#39;`, 5 `&amp;`.)
  - **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)**: cloned a Hero's job — League names row reads `Hero's Tryouts …` on screen and the new league is created with a real apostrophe. **Ann also accepts leaving the 19 existing rows as-is** (age out via clone).

Add any you want under **Configure → Discount Codes**."* Copy only — no logic or flow change. ### AM-078: [Configure / Job Clone → Dropdowns] Grad Years & Recruiting Grad Years dropdowns are NOT advanced on clone (unlike age groups/teams)
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-04) — Job Clone walkthrough
- **Where**: the **Grad Years** and **Recruiting Grad Years** option lists under **Configure → Job Settings → Dropdowns**.
- **What Ann observed**: on a forward clone these two dropdowns should roll forward a year (drop the past year, add the next) — the same way teams and age groups advance — but they don't.
- **Root cause (verified, code)**: the dropdown lists are stored as `List_GradYears` / `List_RecruitingGradYears` inside the job's **`JsonOptions`** JSON blob ([DdlOptionsService.cs:76-77, 109-110](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/DdlOptionsService.cs#L76); loaded via `GetJsonOptionsAsync`, [:31-43](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/DdlOptionsService.cs#L31)). The clone copies `Jobs.JsonOptions` **verbatim** (`CopyScalars`) — nothing shifts it by `yearDelta`. **Inconsistent with** the clone already advancing grad-year *bounds* on age groups (`GradYearMin/Max + 1`, [JobCloneResetRules.cs:586-589](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneResetRules.cs#L586)) and teams ([:778-779](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneResetRules.cs#L778)). Net effect: a cloned job's age-group bounds move to 2027+ while the player-facing Grad Years dropdown still offers 2026.
- **Ann's question**: automate the roll-forward as part of the clone, OR just add a note under "What you need to turn on"?
- **Recommendation (Claude): automate it.** Reasons: (a) it's deterministic — the entries are 4-digit years, so bump each year token by `yearDelta` (leave non-year entries like "Other"/"N/A" alone), reusing the exact tactic already in `IncrementYearsInName`/`BumpYears` ([JobCloneTransforms.cs:47](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneTransforms.cs#L47)); (b) it keeps the clone **internally consistent** with the age-group/team bounds that already advance — otherwise the two contradict each other; (c) a "turn on" note relies on the operator hand-editing two dropdown lists every single clone, which is exactly the toil the year-shift exists to remove. Gate on `yearDelta >= 1` (advance only), same as the name/age-group logic.
- **For Todd (if automating)**: in the clone, parse the new job's `JsonOptions`, bump the year tokens in `List_GradYears` + `List_RecruitingGradYears` by `yearDelta`, re-serialize. Confirm: (a) whether Recruiting Grad Years should shift by the same delta (assume yes); (b) how to treat hand-curated/non-contiguous lists (bumping each entry preserves the shape — safe); (c) leave non-numeric entries untouched. **Fallback if not automating**: add a "Grad Years / Recruiting Grad Years — roll forward under Configure → Job Settings → Dropdowns" bullet to the "What you need to turn on" panel (AM-073/077 region).
- **Severity**: 🟡 Correctness/UX (stale grad-year options on cloned jobs; inconsistent with age-group/team advance)
- **Cross-ref**: AM-075 (same Dates/seed-forward theme), AM-073/077 ("what you need to turn on" panel — home of the fallback note), age-group/team grad-year advance in `JobCloneResetRules`.
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — automated (Todd go, 2026-08-04); confirmed on a rebuilt local API (first miss was a stale binary, not the code). New pure transform `ShiftGradYearOptions` ([JobCloneTransforms.cs:63+](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneTransforms.cs#L63)), called from the Jobs rules block ([JobCloneResetRules.cs:170-179](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.API/Services/Admin/JobCloneResetRules.cs#L170)) so the dropdowns advance with the age-group/team bounds instead of contradicting them.
  - **Deliberately conservative**, because `JsonOptions` is also read by adult registration and the team LOP parse: only `List_GradYears` + `List_RecruitingGradYears` are touched (every other key carried through); only entries that are **entirely** a `20xx` year shift, so `Other`/`N/A`/`2026 (rising)` survive untouched; both `Text` and `Value` shift (legacy rows carry the year in `Value` with `Text` empty — confirmed in the data); writes go back through the blob's **actual** key casing so a variant can't add a duplicate key; **advance-only** (`yearDelta < 1` is a no-op); and **any parse failure returns the original string** — never a partial rewrite, so the worst case is exactly the pre-AM-078 behavior rather than a corrupt blob.
  - **Blast radius**: the `JsonOptions` column on the **newly created job only**. The source job is never written; no existing job changes.
  - **Data probed 2026-08-04 (Staging TSICV5, all 1,066 jobs)**: 1,065 carry a blob and **all 1,065 are valid JSON**; 1,062 have `List_GradYears`, 670 have `List_RecruitingGradYears`; uniform shape `[{"Text":"2028","Value":"2028"},…]`.
  - **Build**: `TSIC.API` clean, 0 warnings / 0 errors. The existing verbatim-copy assertion ([JobCloneServiceTests.cs:435-445](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/backend/TSIC.Tests/JobClone/JobCloneServiceTests.cs#L435)) uses `{"x":1}` with no grad years, so it stays green unchanged. **Todd runs the suite.**
  - **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)**: on a **freshly rebuilt** local API, cloning forward advances Grad Years + Recruiting Grad Years by one year (non-year entries unchanged). *(First test showed no shift — traced to a **stale running binary** (this checkout was 21 commits behind until re-synced); a `dotnet` rebuild + re-clone fixed it. Data confirmed: dates shifted 2027→2028 so `yearDelta` fired, only the pre-rebuild binary lacked `ShiftGradYearOptions`.)*

### AM-079: [Design system / Save affordance] Users forget to click the bottom-right SAVE — reconsider placement or add an unsaved-changes guard
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-04) — general observation across Admin menus (Ann flagged as "not sure about this one")
- **What Ann raised**: on many Admin function menus, when you make changes a **SAVE button appears bottom-right**; she has forgotten to click it several times and lost edits. Would moving it to the **upper-right** (or another option) help?
- **Current implementation (code)**: it's a **shared global sticky bar** — `.detail-save-bar` ([styles.scss:72-105](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/styles.scss#L72)), used across LADT, Configure/Job, DDL Options, Widget Editor, USLax, Scheduling, etc. It's already an *active* affordance, not a static button: `position: sticky; bottom: 0`, **warms to amber when dirty** (`.is-dirty`), shows an "unsaved" hint, and the button **pulses once** on the clean→dirty transition (`ladt-save-pulse`, [:110-117](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/styles.scss#L110)). So the miss isn't invisibility — it's that a passive indicator can still be walked past. (Because the class is global, a *visual* change lands everywhere at once; a nav-guard is per-feature.)
- **Recommendation (Claude)** — prefer a **safety net over relocation**:
  1. **Best: unsaved-changes guard on navigation.** If the user leaves a screen with pending edits, prompt "You have unsaved changes." Solves "I forgot to click Save" directly, wherever the button sits. **Precedent already in the codebase** — the registrant fly-in does this (`showDiscardConfirm` → "Discard unsaved changes?", [registration-detail-panel.component.html:785+](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/search/registrations/components/registration-detail-panel.component.html#L785)). Extend that pattern to the admin menus (per-route/per-panel dirty check → confirm on leave).
  2. **Optional: strengthen the dirty signal** — make the warm/dirty state a touch more persistent than a one-shot pulse (without becoming noisy).
  3. **Relocation (Ann's idea)**: moving to upper-right is a lateral move — a bottom action bar is a standard, thumb-reachable pattern, and a top button can be overlooked just as easily. **Duplicating** top+bottom would cut against **AM-051** ("one Save affordance"). So relocation alone is the weakest of the three; the leave-guard is the real fix.
- **For Todd + Ann to align**: this is a genuine design call (Ann is unsure). Recommend: keep the single bottom bar, add the leave-guard (reuse the fly-in's discard-confirm pattern), optionally firm up the dirty state — rather than relocate. Decide scope (which admin menus first) if approved.
- **Severity**: 🟡 UX / data-loss risk (forgotten saves) — cross-cutting, not a single-screen bug
- **Cross-ref**: AM-051 (one Save affordance), AM-068/069 (Save/Cancel prominence & order), the fly-in `showDiscardConfirm` precedent.
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** for the extended scope (Dropdowns + Widget Editor); guard approach accepted over relocation. **Follow-up open**: other unguarded screens to be filed per-screen as Ann hits them (Profile Editor / Scheduling / browser-close known gaps). — *(Built by Todd 2026-08-04: the guard already existed on Configure → Job; extended to two more editors.)* **Todd's question reframed the item**: "don't we already have a 'you have unsaved data' warning?" We do — `unsavedChangesGuard`, text *"You have unsaved changes. Leave this page?"* ([unsaved-changes.guard.ts:17](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/infrastructure/guards/unsaved-changes.guard.ts#L17)) — but it was wired to **exactly one route in the entire app**, Configure → Job ([app.routes.ts:129](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/app.routes.ts#L129)). So this was never "should we add a warning", it was "the warning is on one screen out of several".
  - **Extended to the other routed editors that own a save bar and already track dirty state**: **Configure → Dropdowns** (`/configure/ddl-options`) and **Configure → Widget Editor**. Each now implements `HasUnsavedChanges` and the route gains `canDeactivate: [unsavedChangesGuard]`. Widget Editor reports dirty for **both** of its editors (the matrix and the per-job override grid) — either can be left pending. Dropdowns' hook only bites on the standalone route; mounted as a Job Settings tab it isn't the routed component and the shell's `dirtyTabs` guard already covers it.
  - **Relocation NOT done, and recommended against**: moving Save to the top-right doesn't help on any screen where nothing catches the user leaving, and Job Settings already demonstrated the guard is what actually prevents the loss. Also cuts against **AM-051** (one Save affordance).
  - **Deliberately not swept** (needs more than guard wiring — raise separately if Ann hits them): **Tools → Profile Editor** has no single aggregate dirty signal, though it does confirm on scope-switch discard; the **Scheduling config panel** tabs hold dirty state inside a panel rather than a routed component. Neither of the two new screens got the `window:beforeunload` handler that Job Settings has ([job-config.component.ts:96-101](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/configure/job/job-config.component.ts#L96)) — route navigation is Ann's reported failure, browser-close is a separate ask.
  - **Typecheck**: `tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — Dropdowns + Widget Editor now confirm before losing unsaved edits on navigate-away. Ann accepts the guard approach over relocation. **She expects the same gap exists on other screens** and will **file specific screens as she hits annoying (unguarded) behavior** — the un-swept set (Profile Editor, Scheduling config panel, browser-close) stays open as a known follow-up, filed per-screen when encountered.

### AM-081: [Configure / LADT → Tree] Team names truncate in the fixed-width tree — add tooltips and let the tree panel widen/expand
- **Source**: Ann (2026-08-04) — LADT walkthrough
- **What Ann saw**: in the LADT Tree, team rows are cut off (e.g. "Queen of LI:Class of 2…", "Play For the Future:Cl…", "Presidents Cup:Class …") with no way to read the full name. Asked whether the tree can be made **expandable to view all options**.
- **Root cause (code)**: the tree lives in a **fixed 280px** panel — `.tree-panel { width: 280px; min-width: 280px }` ([ladt.component.scss:19-21](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.scss#L19)) — and team labels ellipsis-truncate: `.tree-label { text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap }` ([:294-297](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.scss#L294)). Level-0 nodes wrap (`.wrap-label`) but deeper team rows don't. It's an Angular **CDK tree** ([ladt.component.html:102](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.html#L102)); the label span ([:141](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.html#L141)) has **no `title`**, so hover reveals nothing. So this is a width/overflow constraint — the full names are present, just clipped.
- **Recommendation (Claude)** — do the first two together:
  1. **Hover tooltip (cheap, immediate)**: add `[title]="node.name"` (and the club/secondary labels where they truncate) so hovering a clipped row shows the full name. No layout change.
  2. **Resizable / expandable panel (the real ask)**: give `.tree-panel` a drag-to-resize handle (or `resize: horizontal; overflow:auto`) so the Director can widen the tree to read everything, then it stays. Keep the 280px as the min.
  3. *(Alternative, weaker: wrap team labels instead of ellipsis — simplest but makes rows variable-height and can get long with 73+ teams; the tooltip+resize combo is better.)*
- **For Todd**: confirm the resize interaction (drag handle vs. CSS `resize`) and that widening the tree reflows the detail pane cleanly; verify tooltips on all three label variants (`.tree-label`, `.tree-label-primary`, `.tree-label-secondary`). Mobile drawer (`@media max-width:767.98px`, [:547+](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.scss#L547)) already uses `min(90vw,360px)` — leave that path as-is.
- **Severity**: 🟡 UX (can't read full team names in the tree)
- **Cross-ref**: AM-067 (Search/Teams page-size — same "let me see more" theme), LADT editor.
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)** — resize built (tooltip already existed), Todd go 2026-08-04; drag-to-widen + persist + double-click reset confirmed.
  - **Premise correction (the filing was wrong on tooltips)**: the entry said the label span has no `title`, so hover reveals nothing. It does — `.tree-node-btn` carries `[title]="getLevelLabel(node.level) + ': ' + (node.clubName ? node.clubName + ' — ' + node.name : node.name)"` ([ladt.component.html:124-125](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.html#L124)), which covers the plain label AND the club/team two-line variant. Hovering a clipped row already showed the full name, level-qualified. **No tooltip code was added** — a second `title` would have been a duplicate.
  - **Built: the panel resizes.** Ann's real ask. Applied the existing shared **`appResizablePanel`** directive to `.tree-panel` with `panelSide="left"` so the grab handle sits on the tree's right inner edge ([ladt.component.html:31-36](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.html#L31)): `storageKey="ladtTreeWidth"`, default/min **280px** (the old fixed width is now the floor, so nothing gets narrower than today), max **560px**. Width **persists per user** in localStorage and **double-click resets**. Reused rather than hand-rolled — this same screen's detail panel already uses the directive ([:329](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.html#L329)), as do 7 other panels; the handle styling, `col-resize` cursor and reduced-motion handling all come from the shared `.resize-handle` contract in `styles/_flyin.scss`.
  - **Mobile regression caught and closed**: the directive ships a global `@media (max-width: 767.98px) { [appResizablePanel] { width: 100vw !important } }` to stop a stored desktop width leaking onto a phone. Applied blind it would also have stretched the LADT **drawer** to full-bleed and thrown away the deliberate `min(90vw, 360px)` cap. The drawer's mobile width is now `!important` to defend it ([ladt.component.scss:551-563](../../TSIC-Core-Angular/src/frontend/tsic-app/src/app/views/ladt/editor/ladt.component.scss#L551)). `.tree-panel` also gains `position: relative` to anchor the injected handle.
  - Template + SCSS only — no TS change (the directive was already in the component's `imports`).
  - **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 2026-08-04)**: drag-to-widen reveals full team names, width persists on reload, double-click snaps back to 280; mobile drawer unaffected.
